# NCM Online — idea ledger (append-only)

> **STATUS: LEDGER** - living and append-only, current by construction. Covers: *the idea ledger - every idea, with permanent IDs* (L68)
> Authority map: [INDEX.md](INDEX.md) - classified 2026-09-24 (L125)

**This file is never pruned.** Every idea, feature, concern, fix and "wouldn't it be good if" goes in here the
moment it is spoken, with a permanent ID, whether or not it will ever be built (L68). Rows change *state*; they are
never deleted. Dropping an idea means setting its state to `dropped` with a reason, so the reasoning survives too.

Capturing is not committing. A `raw` row costs nothing and obliges nothing. The point is that a caffeinated human
and an agent with no memory can both come back in three weeks and find the thought intact.

**States:** `raw` (captured, no design) → `shaped` (there is a design or an owner answer) → `scheduled` (assigned to
a milestone or batch) → `built` (code + offline proof) → `validated` (live PASS logged) · `dropped (reason)` ·
`blocked (on what)`.

**Where things live:** ideas and work → here. Choices made and questions for the owner → `DECISION_RECORD.md`.
Defects → `KNOWN_BUGS.md`. Live PASS/FAIL → `wp/MASTER_TEST.md`. What to do next → `NOW.md`.

**IDs are permanent.** Next free ID: **IDEA-516**.
*(This line has now been stale twice — left at IDEA-127 while the ledger ran to IDEA-183, then at IDEA-205 while it
ran to IDEA-294. It is the only guard against reusing an ID. Check it against the last row before you append.)*

## Latest intake - 2026-09-26

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IDEA-515 | **ENG** | 2026-09-26 | **A native-asset Foundry may need no new 3D models: treat the game's existing vehicle families, player records, tuned/quest records and appearances as a provenanced source catalogue, then mint NCM-owned machine identities and configurations from validated combinations.** | owner, 2026-09-26: *"foundry, without a single new 3d model"* | **RAW -- inventory and feasibility audit owed; no catalogue expansion authorised** | vehicle catalogue; Foundry; Machine provenance; staged releases | The current NCM pool is only an authored racing subset: 26 native conceptual machines (21 four-wheel identities and five motorcycles), plus the external RB15. The installed current game exposes 86 record names matching `Vehicle.v_*..._player`, but that is not 86 unique meshes and it mixes base-game, free-update, expansion, quest, tuned, weaponized and appearance variants. The audit must therefore inventory **record -> underlying entity/appearance -> content package -> conceptual platform**, distinguish a release from a repeat mint, and validate spawn, driving, handling, damage, doors/seats, AI racing and client content availability before a record enters the canonical catalogue. NCM fictional manufacturer/model/serial identity is a separate presentation and provenance layer; it does not make CDPR geometry redistributable outside the game or make an expansion-backed record safe for a base-game-only client. Scheduled maintenance and drip-fed economy release are compatible with this direction, but neither substitutes for the runtime validation or package-entitlement gates. |

## How this file is organised (2026-09-24)

Three changes, no deletions. Every row, ID and word of every note is still here.

1. **Every row now carries a `Class`** — what *kind* of thing it is, which decides how expensive it is to get
   wrong later. This is a different axis from `State`, which says how far along it is.
2. **Completed rows moved to the bottom**, under their original section headings, in `## Completed`.
3. **The foundation register below** names what has to be right *before* production takes more data.

| Class | Meaning | Cost of changing it later |
|---|---|---|
| **F0** | Substrate that production **already stores**, or an owner decision gating it — identity, serialisation, provenance, immutable history | **Highest.** A migration at best; permanently lost history at worst |
| **F1** | Data model the vision depends on, **not yet written** — parts, components, engineering, wear | Free to design today. A migration the moment any production row exists |
| **INV** | An invariant: a rule that constrains the model rather than code that implements it | Cheap now, structural later — these decide what F1 is allowed to look like |
| **GATE** | A proof or audit that must pass before something can safely be built on top | Cheap. Skipping one is what makes the others expensive |
| **SYS** | System behaviour and logic | Low — changeable without migrating data |
| **UI** | Presentation, HUD, panels, content, ceremony | Lowest. Deferrable indefinitely |
| **OPS** | Tooling, process, docs, QA harness | Low |
| **UP** | Blocked on the platform / upstream ask | Not ours to schedule |
| **DSN** | Open product or design question with no data implication yet | Free |

`·LIVE` appended to a class means **production already holds rows in that shape**.

---

## ⚑ Foundation register — what must be right before production takes more data

The owner's framing, 2026-09-24: *"we can't slip up on things like serialized cars… those serializations need to
exist in their relative final form before touching production anymore… I'd rather be parked at the door for design
work later than find out after building on weak foundation."*

**The audit says that instinct is correct, and one item is already costing us.** 35 of the 163 open rows are
data-model or schema work; 28 are UI that can wait indefinitely. The split is real, not a feeling.

### P0 — decide or build before the next production race

- **IDEA-236** — F0 — **This one is actively losing history right now.** Production holds **29 completed results, none attributable to a machine.** `session_result` has no `asset_id` — and neither does `entry` or `seat`, so it is **not reconstructible**: the car fielded was never written down anywhere. Every race run from now adds another permanently anonymous result. It also blocks the Machine Profile (`IDEA-237`) and undercuts wear/engineering, which need to know *which machine* did the distance. Cheap to add, impossible to backfill.
- **IDEA-233** — F0 — Owner decision: do admin/progression grants carry serials? Until answered, the serial space has an undefined hole and every grant made meanwhile is a row that may need retrofitting.
- **IDEA-238** — F0 — Owner decision: are NCX team cars persistent assets? Today they are equipment with no `assetId`, so they cannot accrue odometer (`IDEA-232`) and cannot carry components. Answer changes the data model for team equipment.
- **IDEA-287** — GATE — Already promoted to prerequisite (L120): odometer integrity must be proven **before** distance carries economic weight. §AG5/§AG6 are still NOT RUN. Once distance is money, a measurement artifact becomes an exploit.
- **IDEA-249** — GATE — Engine-capability audit — *gates every component effect*. Building a parts model before knowing what vehicle behaviour can actually be manipulated risks a model describing things the engine cannot express.

### P1 — the parts/engineering/wear data model, while it is still free

Nothing here has a production row yet, which is exactly why now is the cheap moment. The spine, in dependency order:

- **IDEA-240** — Vehicle mechanical architecture profile — compatibility *derived*, not listed per model
- **IDEA-241** — Component **design** identity vs the manufactured component **asset** — the same offering/asset split already proven live by `IDEA-213`
- **IDEA-286** — The seven layers that must never merge on one component
- **IDEA-283** — Seeded lifecycle profile evaluated against accumulated exposure — no per-km rolls
- **IDEA-246** / **IDEA-247** — Parts ownable independently of vehicles; atomic heterogeneous barter (the largest new transaction architecture implied anywhere in this ledger)
- **IDEA-254** — Distribution relationships at four scopes — explicitly schema-shaping
- **IDEA-276** — Attributed operation history on component assets (the engineering biography)
- **IDEA-266** — Knowledge ladder, stored separately from bounded competency (L120)

The **14 INV rows** are not work — they are the rules that decide what the above is allowed to look like. They cost
nothing now and become structural once a table exists. Settle them alongside the model, not after it.

### Deliberately NOT gating anything

All 28 **UI** rows, and the presentation half of the parts specs (`IDEA-248` product cards, `IDEA-253` market
switching, `IDEA-277` roll ceremony, `IDEA-237` Machine Profile). They need the model; the model does not need them.

| IDEA-050 | **UI** | 2026-09-21 | **Braking-zone markers along the racing line, native to NCM.** Owner runs a third-party in-world navigation mod modified to paint red braking zones and asked whether it could be server-side. It cannot (L71) — but NCM can do it better, because NCM owns the route and the telemetry the mod never had. Two derivations: (a) geometric — curvature of the authored route plan gives a braking point per corner per class; (b) empirical — 5 Hz client telemetry already carries brake, throttle, speed and slip, so the brake-application positions of the fastest recorded laps *are* the braking zones, and they improve as people drive. ## In-world route lines (2026-09-21 — owner's question, promoted to a design direction)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IDEA-053 | **SYS** | 2026-09-21 | **Paint the racing line from a recorded lap, not a computed path — and stream only a rolling window of it.** The hard part of an in-world line is making it adhere to the road between sparse race nodes; every *computed* answer runs into the engine's own road planner, which is the thing already failing us (KB-1 waypoint painting, KB-11 `driveTo` stalls). So do not compute it. **Drive the route once and keep the positions:** the 5 Hz telemetry stream already records position, and every sample was taken by a car actually on the road, so the polyline is road-adhering by construction and each point carries a true ground z (which also retires the KB-17 class of spawn-height bugs). Simplify with Douglas–Peucker to a few hundred points, store as `line[]` on the route in the pack beside `gates` and the AI path, and version it with the route revision. **Render as a rolling window**, not the whole route: only the next ~200–300 m ahead of the player, markers recycled as they pass. A 6630 m route at one marker per 10 m would be 663 props — far past anything E19 has measured — whereas a 300 m window is ~30 and constant regardless of route length. **Braking zones come free from the same recording** (IDEA-050): the lap already carries brake, throttle, speed and slip per sample, so brake-application positions are in the data — colour those markers red. **This is also the answer to waypoint weirdness (IDEA-052):** an in-world line the player follows makes the minimap road route a nice-to-have instead of a dependency. | owner question + agent, 2026-09-21 | shaped | design pass, then with E19/E24 | Authoring already exists (`/ncm.au`, `/ncm.aw`) and so does the telemetry. Constraints: **E19** unrun (prop quota / FPS — the rolling window is the mitigation, and makes E19 a much easier experiment); **KB-16** — markers carry no labels or every one sprouts an "E" card; alias must come from the curated table (**KB-14**), `race.route_arrow` is the obvious candidate. Open: does a recorded line need one capture per class (a Formula line differs from a street line), or one geometric line with per-class braking points? |
| IDEA-054 | **F1** | 2026-09-21 | **Telemetry retention tiers — decide before the data grows.** Owner: telemetry should be available post-race, "although that invites discussion on what we store versus store for awhile vs for a little while vs just race." Proposed shape: **(a) session-hot** — full 5 Hz samples, kept days, powers post-race analysis and incident review; **(b) derived-warm** — per-sector splits, a simplified best-lap line and brake points, kept for the season, powers IDEA-053/050 and coaching; **(c) permanent** — route records, best times, high scores, box scores, which the owner wants "once we're mature". Deleting (a) must never damage (b) or (c), so the derivation runs at settlement, not on read. | owner, 2026-09-21 | raw | before records go live (M9/M10) | `telemetry_chunk` already accumulates at roughly raceSeconds/10 rows per driver per session; on a public server that is the first table to become a problem. |
| IDEA-055 | **UI** | 2026-09-21 | **Live timing graph needs UI polish.** Visible by default and functionally working during the race (1.3 PASS); the owner wants it to "look right". Not a defect — a design pass on the strip. | owner, 2026-09-21 | raw | TW2 readiness |  |

## World feel, gear and the economy (2026-09-21 — owner, during the guide test)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IDEA-056 | **SYS** | 2026-09-21 | **Weather as server policy, not showcase.** OPEN//77's default cycles weather fast to show it off; a motorsport world wants weather that holds long enough to matter and changes for sporting reasons. Per-bucket time/weather already exists (`Open77.environment … bucket`), so an event can declare its conditions and a world can run a slow, plausible cycle. | owner, 2026-09-21 | **built; live validation pending** | current Weather slice | Architecture: **[11_WEATHER.md](11_WEATHER.md)**. Platform gate passed in **[E26](archive/experiments/E26_2026-09-22_weather-sync.md)**. Core plus migration 013/default-world adapter are offline green; §X must validate persistence, freeze/application, read model and restart continuity before the service is called live. |
| IDEA-057 | **UP** | 2026-09-21 | **Wet grip, by the lever that actually exists.** True weather-reactive tyre physics needs runtime code on the client, which the `Open77.*` boundary does not allow (no engine RTTI), and per-instance handling is category **A**. Two things *are* available: (a) a **server-driven wet ceiling** — drop `Open77.vehicles.setPerformance` ceilings for the session when conditions are wet, which is replicated, fair, and server-authoritative, giving wet races a real and consistent character without touching physics; (b) an **inert global handling tweak** (TweakXL/.tweak/INI) shipped as required content, which is static and cannot react to weather. Owner has prior C++ engine experience (clutch/coast work on a manual-transmission mod) and does not need it to be perfect. | owner idea + agent, 2026-09-21 | shaped | after KB-6; E9 | Hard prerequisite: **KB-6**. Unsynced weather plus grip effects means two drivers race under different rules without knowing it — worse than no wet racing. |
| IDEA-058 | **SYS** | 2026-09-21 | **Dress the driver on arrival, and make gear a thing.** Everyone who spawns gets clothed by default rather than arriving in whatever the save had; driver gear (helmets, racesuits, sourced from Nexus) becomes part of identity and later of the economy. | owner, 2026-09-21 | **built first slice — live validation pending; larger system shaped in `09_APPAREL.md`** | M2.3 / TW2 readiness | Starter bridge fills only missing shirt/trousers/shoes through OPEN//77's authoritative persistence. Clothing archives are inert in principle, but modded gear remains gated by KB-8, item rights, and a missing public custom-catalogue registration hook. |
| IDEA-059 | **DSN** | 2026-09-21 | **Answer the economy's first questions before building its numbers.** Why do you own a car? Who gives you your first one on arrival? What *is* the default intro experience? These are prior to prices and balance (Q20/IDEA-012) and they decide the shape of the world layer: L13 already provides the mechanisms (`issued`, `loaner`, `owned`, `byo`) — what is missing is the authored answer to which one a new arrival meets, and why. | owner, 2026-09-21 | shaped | design pass, feeds M2/M10 | Directly load-bearing for the soft launch (L66): the first ten minutes of a stranger's visit is exactly this question. |
| IDEA-060 | **DSN** | 2026-09-21 | **Two roads into NCX: the pay driver and the recruited driver.** A street racer who never touches the sanctioned ladder but becomes a legend and earns enough can simply *buy* a ludicrously expensive NCX car and enter; another arrives through merit, seats and team recruitment. Both are legitimate. This is the idea that gives the economy a purpose, gives street racing a stake in Formula, and makes L58's "two tiers, both first-class" mean something mechanically instead of tonally — the tiers become connected by money and reputation rather than merely coexisting. Rulesets already carry a provisioning model and eligibility per series (L13), so a seat obtained by purchase and a seat obtained by recruitment can differ in *standing* (works entry vs privateer) while racing the same car. | owner, 2026-09-21 | shaped | design pass; M10 economy switch | The most structurally valuable idea of the session — it is the missing link between the two products. Open: does a bought entry score for a championship, or only for results and records? Does a works team resent you? |

## The world around the racing (2026-09-21 — owner, submitted as catalog entries, not work orders)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IDEA-061 | **DSN** | 2026-09-21 | **The game between the races — NCM motorsport culture.** *Open product/design question, not a feature.* What does a player do when not racing? The loop must not collapse to join → race → race → leave, and equally must not drift into a generic Cyberpunk RP server where racing is one activity among many. Intended centre of gravity (directional, not requirements): ~50% racing, ~30–35% general driving/automotive life, ~15–20% social/culture/garages/preparation — i.e. **80–85% stays automotive**. *Core principle:* **NCM keeps noticing you when you drive outside a race**, without sporting penalties and without making free roam stressful — distance driven, personal top speed, average speed, clean distance, impacts as non-punitive observations, distance at night, distance in rain, class usage, venues visited, long journeys, starts/finishes/wins/podiums/time trials, street vs sanctioned — accumulating into a **motoring biography**. Not every observation earns a reward or a popup; no XP confetti. *Identity:* achievements unlock **cosmetic identity only** — badges, titles, banner components, stickers/emblems, profile decoration — never performance. They should communicate history: another player sees it and knows you did something. (Mario Kart World free-roam stickers/banners are loose inspiration, not a template.) *Culture:* encourage lightweight organic RP without requiring formal RP, via physical anchors — garages, workshops, paddocks, car meets, dealerships/showrooms, motorsport HQ/sanctioning locations, racing-scene bars/clubs, team spaces much later. Many need little bespoke gameplay: concentrating useful automotive functions in recognisable places may be enough to make people congregate. *The Race Guide is load-bearing here:* with a race 10–20 minutes out, the answer must not be "disconnect and come back". *Research task for later:* study FiveM/GTA racing communities and comparable automotive servers for what players do between races, where they gather, how culture emerges, progression/identity, garages/meets, how much formal RP is required vs emergent, and what keeps a racing server inhabited — as comparative research, not to copy. | owner, 2026-09-21 | **raw (open design question)** | design exploration; no milestone yet | Supersedes and absorbs **IDEA-005** (Q22 "what exists between races"), which is now the thin version of this. Cross-refs: **IDEA-059** (who gives you your first car / intro experience), **IDEA-060** (pay driver — gives the street tier somewhere to lead), **IDEA-002** (three-door rule — "not engaging yet" is a first-class choice), **IDEA-003/004** (self-sustaining server, empty-server run), **IDEA-054** (retention: a motoring biography is a *derived* tier, not raw samples), **L58** (two tiers, both first-class). |
| IDEA-062 | **SYS** | 2026-09-21 | **Live server weather + motorsport forecast.** Weather is authoritative world state, not an event preset. A deterministic rolling canonical plan supports slower conditions and planning across roughly seven real days; a separate confidence-aware forecast reveals less certainty with lead time. Presentation is temporal pit-wall instrumentation, never fake geographic radar. | owner, 2026-09-21 | **specified; platform gate validated** | next Weather slice | Full architecture: **[11_WEATHER.md](11_WEATHER.md)**. Platform synchronization passed in **[E26](archive/experiments/E26_2026-09-22_weather-sync.md)**. Race Guide, immutable event provenance, Driving Ledger, Fishing and future wet-performance authority remain consumers of the not-yet-built shared service. |

Cyberpunk gives a fictionally coherent way to build NCM's own version through quickhacks, defences and self-boosts rather than shells and bananas. **This could be an NCM signature.** But sanctioned/NCX motorsport stays protected: stewarded racing and chaotic street competition must coexist. | owner, 2026-09-21 | **shaped — CONFIRMED FEASIBLE 2026-09-21; roster still deliberately unstarted** | no milestone | **`onHackingTransition` is the completion hook** (phases incl. `impact`, `effect_applied`), so NCM composes the vehicle consequence itself — IDEA-067 is real. **But `open77_hacking` is not loaded on staging** (IDEA-074). Names and rules are placeholders; the architectural idea is the separation itself — **a player may own powerful cyberware and still not be allowed to use it in this race.** **(J) Weapons** follow the same rule: do not strip Cyberpunk's weapon world out of NCM merely because NCM is motorsport, but possession and race legality are separate questions. | owner, 2026-09-21 | shaped | no milestone | Fits the existing architecture almost exactly: rulesets already carry format, penalties, consequences, provisioning, eligibility, allowed backends and topology policy per series (L24, `rules/rulesets`). A notorious hacker becomes notorious because humans recognise the behaviour — leading to refused challenges, players seeking them out, organic rivalries, informal street justice. ## Chrome roster — first design pass (2026-09-21, owner)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-076** | **SYS** | 2026-09-21 | **PARENT — NCM Chrome automotive quickhack roster, design pass v0.** Full reasoning in **[06_CHROME_ROSTER.md](06_CHROME_ROSTER.md)**. Principle: *create a driving problem, do not replace driving with a punishment animation.* The platform's limits are the design — 80 m range, timed warned interruptible uploads, ICE, purge, ally purge, safe-area — so landing a hack requires racecraft and the victim can usually see the attacker in their mirrors. Six categories: offense, self-enhancement, defense, recovery, utility/deception, extreme. Working roster: **LIMITER** (performance debuff, the foundational hack), **ENGINE CUT**, **LIMP/system failure** (concept, caution), **TIRE SABOTAGE** (persistent mechanical consequence), **POWER SURGE** (boost that doubles as range escape), **OVERCLOCK** (slot only), **SELF-ICE**, **PURGE**, **ALLY PURGE**, **SYSTEM RESTORE**, **BLACKOUT**, and a gated **extreme/catastrophic** tier ending in vehicle destruction. Explicitly *not* ordinary attacks: `setFrozen`, `undrivable`, person-freeze/knockdown. **Break or degrade the machine; do not steal the steering wheel.** Nothing balanced; no values locked. | owner, 2026-09-21 | **shaped (design pass v0)** | no milestone — implementation gated on IDEA-077's prerequisites | Verified against CAPABILITY_PALETTE.md. Four technical findings recorded in the doc, of which **the ceiling collision (IDEA-079) is load-bearing**. Cross-refs IDEA-064–075 throughout; ruleset tiers per IDEA-068; fingerprint per IDEA-069/070; ally purge gated on IDEA-075. |
| IDEA-077 | **SYS** | 2026-09-21 | **First playable Chrome vertical slice — four mechanics only: LIMITER, POWER SURGE, SELF-ICE, PURGE.** That set alone yields attack → chase → escape attempt → range management → boost → ICE block → purge → cooldown → re-engagement. **If that loop is fun between two humans, expand the roster; if it is not, the roster does not matter.** | owner, 2026-09-21 | shaped | after TW2; not before | **Hard prerequisites:** the ceiling authority (IDEA-079), `open77_hacking` loaded (IDEA-074), the hold-F8 key collision settled (IDEA-074/046), and a presentation decision (roster doc finding 3). |
| IDEA-078 | **SYS** | 2026-09-21 | **Notoriety artifact — the skull badge.** Destroying another human's vehicle through an eligible Chrome mechanic becomes a permanent part of behavioural history and earns a displayable badge (working name: death skull). **Tone: NCM is not a moralising nanny.** Not "BAD PERSON −100 MORALITY" — closer to "this player has done this". The player may be proud of it and display it deliberately; others read it as *do not trust them* or as *finally, someone fun*. The delicious case is the contradiction: bubblegum-pink car, wholesome banner, cute badges — and an enormous Chrome/aggression fingerprint with a skull on it. **Something is not right here.** Players decide what that means. | owner, 2026-09-21 | shaped | with IDEA-069/070 | The curated-vs-immutable split (IDEA-070) is exactly what makes this work: presentation is chosen, history is not. |
| **IDEA-079** | **GATE** | 2026-09-21 | **THE CEILING AUTHORITY — one owner of `setPerformance` per vehicle.** `setPerformance` is **one profile per vehicle and does not stack**, yet NCM wants it for class/homologation ceilings (E9), wet conditions (IDEA-057), LIMITER debuffs and POWER SURGE boosts — four writers, one slot. Worse, `clearPerformance` "hands a vehicle back its record's rated performance", so clearing a hack would **blow past the class cap and reward the victim with an illegal car**. Needs one module holding the contributing layers and writing the composed result, with nothing else permitted to call `setPerformance`. | agent, 2026-09-21 | **shaped — build before any of the above** | before E9, IDEA-057 or IDEA-077 | Useful on its own for class ceilings and wet racing even if Chrome never ships. The cheapest possible time to build it is now, while exactly one caller exists. |

## Visual identity, assets and the driving ledger (2026-09-21 evening — art session)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-081** | **F0** | 2026-09-21 | **THE DRIVING LEDGER — an always-on odometer. Highest-leverage engineering item on the art side.** NCM measures nothing outside a session: `telemetry_chunk` is per-session at 5 Hz and free-roam driving is recorded **nowhere**. So **~11 badges depend on one missing subsystem**: Distance, Night Owl, Early Bird, Sun Seeker, Rain Racer, Storm Chaser, Desert/Coastal/City/Highway Run, Off Track, High Altitude. Needs: sampled position/speed/time-of-day/weather/region while driving, folded into durable per-driver aggregates (never raw retention, per L74b). **This is IDEA-061's "NCM keeps noticing you when you drive outside a race" made concrete** — build it and a third of the badge tree becomes derivable. | agent, 2026-09-21 | **shaped — build before the distance badge family** | with IDEA-061 | Cross-refs IDEA-054 (retention: aggregates survive, samples do not), IDEA-062 (rain/weather distance needs KB-6 fixed first), L74. |
| **IDEA-080** | **UI** | 2026-09-21 | **Asset contract + UI surface map.** Produce now: NCM/NCX/RC marks (light + icon variants, L72), tier and discipline glyphs, race-state glyphs, the system-glyph sheet, badges for existing data keys, 8 NCX team badges, 3–5 banners as a crop test, and — **highest leverage of all — a bundled OFL webfont**, because the CSS currently asks for Rajdhani and falls back to whatever each client happens to have installed, so NCM looks different on every machine. Wait: per-hack Chrome art, garage/tuning, weather art, stickers/rarity frames, exploration badges. Formats: SVG masters on a 24-grid with a single-colour `currentColor` fallback; multicolour is allowed where colour carries stable identity or materially improves recognition, using a controlled CSS palette—interactive state colours are never baked. Banners 3:1 at 1536×512 master shipping ≤768×256, masters kept outside `web/`. States are CSS, never baked. Manifest row per asset with `bindsTo` — and **`bindsTo: null` for glyphs whose feature does not exist yet** (crew, voice, friends, spectate, photo mode, replay), so nobody later assumes a feature exists because there is an icon for it. | coder 1 + agent, 2026-09-21 | **built (first live-bound tranche; live validation pending)** | asset production | Hard constraints: 1920×1080 fixed canvas, no DPI scaling; `web_files` only, no CDN; native surfaces take text + a ≤4-char short code only; the Driver Card is drawn per remote player per frame (~240×80, no blur). First tranche binds Guide tier/route/interest, venue actions, and race HUD formation/status/finish to `web/glyphs.js`; broader sheet, marks, font, badges, teams and banners remain. Offline proof 2026-09-22: all WebUI scripts parse, manifest parses, 96 core tests green under Lua 5.4 + LuaJIT, 89 resource files `luac -p` clean. |
| **IDEA-082** | **UI** | 2026-09-21 | **Banner and badge prestige system.** Ladder **BRONZE → SILVER → GOLD → DIAMOND → PURPLE**, cumulative career behaviour, never downgrades. ~40 badges × 5 tiers ≈ 150 authored reward moments from ~40 marks + 5 frames + 6 ceremony packages. Ceremony belongs to the **tier**, not the badge; the reveal always shows what you had first. Unique badges (First Race, First Win, All Regions, Rare Find, Secret, Special Event, True Driver) do not prestige. **Secret is a family, not one badge** — a visual category for hidden conditions. **Special Event becomes dated souvenirs** ("TW2 SHOWCASE — you were there"): scarcity by historical fact, no store, no FOMO rotation. 12 launch banners with a **deliberately dull default**, so the first unlock reads as an upgrade. Flavour text is per badge per tier and already authored. | owner, 2026-09-21 | shaped | after IDEA-081 for the distance family | Invariants in **L74**. ## Race Tags and the first multiplayer race (2026-09-22)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-089** | **SYS** | 2026-09-22 | **POST-EVENT DESCRIPTORS ("Race Tags") — replaces the `Clean Race` accomplishment's job at the end of an event.** **Not** badges, achievements, unlocks, titles, rewards or progression: ephemeral classifications generated from the telemetry of the event that just ended, answering *"what kind of race did I just have?"*. A player may receive the same descriptor every race forever. **Language rule: immediately understandable from the words alone** — `Clean Race`, `Good Start`, `Many Overtakes`, `Close Finish` yes; `Lights Out`, `Metronome`, `Sector One Demon` no. A little personality is fine only where meaning stays obvious (`Tough One`). Every descriptor must be **factually supported by telemetry** — no vague praise. **Mode-correct language: a time trial gets `Clean Run`, never `Clean Race`.** Show **2–4 maximum** in one compact post-event presentation, most distinctive first; **never one toast per descriptor**. No collection screen, no ownership, no prestige, no unlock animation, no completion tracking — that is badge territory. Telemetry stays canonical; descriptors are one-event interpretations of it. Owner's framing: **"they aren't little awards, they're labels on the tape."** Presentation target: `RACE COMPLETE · P4` / `CLEAN RACE · GOOD START · MANY OVERTAKES`. Ceremonial but small, then gone; counts may be logged to lifetime profile stats (e.g. *all-time clean races: N*). Draft bank of ~45 descriptors authored across cleanliness, start, positions, overtaking, pace, consistency, battle, lead, recovery, difficulty, incidents, damage, penalties, route, execution, result, time trial and practice. **Ruthlessly delete any descriptor telemetry cannot support cleanly** — `Strong Defence` and `Good Recovery` are named as the likely first cuts, because plain language does not rescue a mushy inference. | owner, 2026-09-22 | specified (packet B-089 v1, 2026-09-22) | with the post-race beat (IDEA-051); brick B-089 | **Resolves KB-35's naming question by replacing the mechanism**: `clean_race` stops being an accomplishment and becomes a descriptor, with the lifetime count living in profile stats. Distinguish `Gained Positions` (can come from DNFs/penalties) from `Many Overtakes` (actual passes) — they are different facts. Cross-refs IDEA-054 (descriptors are derived at settlement, not recomputed), IDEA-082 (badges stay the long-term layer), L74. **Assignment packet:** [bricks/assignments/B-089-race-tags.v1.md](bricks/assignments/B-089-race-tags.v1.md) — v1 descriptor set limited to what settled rows + gate crossings support; overtakes/defence/recovery explicitly declined until position-over-time facts exist. |
| IDEA-090 | **SYS** | 2026-09-22 | **Solo/wall incidents may be too expensive for a *safety* rating.** The first two-human race cost −0.80 and −0.90 SR, entirely from `kind=object` contact with walls — nobody touched anybody. The arithmetic is correct (0.1/band), but a rating whose job is to say *"is this driver safe to race alongside"* arguably should weight car-to-car contact far above scenery. Options: separate scales, a lower object coefficient, or a distinct "car control" dimension so wall damage lands on the Fingerprint without gutting SR. | agent, 2026-09-22 | raw | with IDEA-069 / SR tuning | Not a bug — a balance question surfaced by real data. Also relevant to Survivor/No Damage badges. |

## Component gate — manufacturing bricks outside the repository (2026-09-22)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-093** | **OPS** | 2026-09-22 | **COMPONENT GATE + integration pipeline.** An external author with no repository access manufactures small, isolated, offline-tested ncm-core modules ("bricks"); the repo-aware coder reviews, admits, wires, deploys and validates. **Finding: the gate already exists** — `ncm/core` + `NcmCore.define` + `COMPAT.md` + `tests/run.lua` is a pure, host-agnostic, dual-interpreter module contract, and `ncm-test.ps1 -CoreRoot` already runs any folder shaped like a core root. So a brick is "a core module not yet in `MANIFEST.lua`", delivered as a runnable mini core root with provided read-only copies of its dependencies. Three shapes only: Rule `(facts, opts) -> result`, Reducer `(state, event, ctx) -> state, deltas`, Catalogue (tables + validate). Host adapters, WebUI, schema and orchestration are never bricks. Contract, packet, passport and review formats in [bricks/](archive/bricks/COMPONENT_GATE.md); queue at `ncm/bricks/` (never read by the build tool, so a candidate cannot ship by accident). Pipeline maps onto the existing ledger: `raw → shaped → specified (packet) → candidate (B-nnn vN) → revision (Rk) → built → validated`; **code existing never advances an idea past candidate** (L69). | owner + agent, 2026-09-22 | shaped | P54 | **Gate-readiness: READY WITHOUT REPO CHANGES.** **Pilot ranking by workflow suitability (not product value):** **1. IDEA-089 Race Tags** — Rule; pure `(facts) -> tags`; input is the settled `classify()` row + incidents + crossings, all of which exist; one call site (`race.lua complete()`, after progression, before announce); removable in one line. Caveat: overtakes/start-reaction facts do not exist yet, so v1 must decline those descriptors — a good test of the "absent ≠ zero" rule. **2. IDEA-082 badge ladder evaluator** — Reducer `(career, event) -> career, crossings`; deterministic tier maths; the "derivable today" subset (Clean Driver, Podium, Top 10, Grinder, Event Attendee, Rival, Chaos Agent) needs only settlement rows; ceremony/presentation stays host. **3. IDEA-081 driving-ledger reducer** — Reducer `(aggregates, sample) -> aggregates`; distance/night/top-speed are derivable from `{t,x,y,z,v}`; region and weather axes must wait (IDEA-084, KB-6), so the packet must scope them out. **Not a brick: IDEA-090** (SR object-vs-car weighting) — a two-line change inside `driver.progression.apply`, cheaper as a core edit with a fixture than as a packet. |

Owner's shape, and the constraints are the design: **a short list of options, three or four maximum**; **each option is a final point, not a branch** — selecting it does the thing; **explicitly not a tree** and not a sprawling menu; **hold-E engages the highlighted option**. Working set: `PRACTICE`, `ENTER RACE`, `HISTORY`. The list is contextual — an option that cannot apply right now is absent, not greyed out and not a dead end (`LEAVE LIST` replaces `ENTER RACE` once you are on it). Not two options in the list and not a sub-menu: the option is final, the panel it opens has both readings. This is also the first surface where `routeRecordSec` / `previousBestSec` (the facts B-089 declines to invent) become visible to a player, and the natural home for KB-31's practice leaderboard now that chat stops being a display surface. |

IDEA-079's ceiling authority now has its first real customer and a committed number to build against, which means it can be specified before the Chrome roster needs it. |

## First live QA of the venue surface (2026-09-22)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-102** | **GATE** | 2026-09-22 | **A ceiling authority, now that there is a real ceiling to authorise.** D7 shipped the first working class ceiling (RB15 at 355 km/h, published on `GlobalState.ncmCeilings`, applied client-side per vehicle record). That makes IDEA-079's problem concrete rather than theoretical: **LIMITER, POWER SURGE, wet grip and the class ceiling all write the same governor slot**, and the last writer wins. Today only one writer exists, so nothing is broken — but the second one silently breaks the first. Needed before any Chrome hack that touches performance: one owner of the slot, per-vehicle, that composes a base class ceiling with temporary modifiers and can always restore the base. **Also unresolved:** `open77_admin` runs its own governor with its own authoritative mirror, so a host using the admin panel's performance tab can fight NCM's ceiling. Nobody has tested what happens when both write. | agent, 2026-09-22, while implementing D7 | **SHAPED; Gate 6 proof owed (`IDEA-435/436`)** | before any performance-affecting Chrome hack (IDEA-076) | Historical note: the client record-class path then had no useful Lua read-back (`TryGetPerformanceProfile` was not bound), strengthening the one-owner rule. **Current official server API now exposes per-instance `getPerformance(id)` / `get(id).performance`; Report 33 records the change.** Read-back verifies delivery but does not make multiple policy writers safe. |

Ship with a core test asserting every `startNodeId`/`endNodeId` on every root resolves, because today a typo is only discovered when the calendar tries to post. | owner, 2026-09-22 ("can we redeploy with all 10 routes… should be called ukulele") | **BUILT 2026-09-22, offline green, undeployed** (L69: not done until a live PASS) | deploy 11; QA §K | Open: `B-line` and `Hold Your Breath` carry `recommendedMode = "drag"` and would run as sprints in this series. Server validates: ids exist, both `raceLegal`, not identical, driver present, not already in a live session (KB-37), sane separation. Owner's constraint recorded: arbitrary player routes must never contaminate the official Race Guide or calendar. |


## Chained routes and concurrent racing (2026-09-22, owner)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-107** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **A route is an ORDERED NODE CHAIN; `A→B` is the two-node case.** Owner: *"Do not create separate architecture for A→B and A→B→C→D."* **SP already worked this way and the Online port narrowed it** — `modules/roots.lua` stores `nodeIds` as canonical and *derives* `startNodeId = nodeIds[1]`, `endNodeId = nodeIds[#nodeIds]`, with `pathDistance` summing legs, consecutive-duplicate collapsing, `isLoop = nodeCount > 2 and first == last`, and per-index resolution errors. Online kept only the derived pair. **So this is restoring a capability, not inventing one.** Model: `data.roots` rows gain `nodes = { … }` as canonical; `startNodeId`/`endNodeId` become derived compatibility accessors so the ten existing roots and every current call site keep working as two-entry chains. `resolve` returns `nodes` + `legs` and sums `distanceM`. **`session.race`, the calendar, progression and the guide need no change** — they already work from `gatesTotal` and `crossings`. Full reasoning: [07_NODE_NETWORK.md](07_NODE_NETWORK.md) Addendum A. | owner, 2026-09-22 + agent audit | **audited, not implemented** | before ephemeral chains (IDEA-106) | **Chain nodes and gates are different things and both are needed.** A node is a curated, named, permanent place — it says *where the route goes*, gets a GPS leg and a split. A gate is an anonymous point that says *which road* — it stops you cutting. Today Online has gates-without-nodes (authored `intermediate` points); SP had nodes-without-gates. The full model wants gates to belong **to a leg**. A chain node compiles to a gate, so legs and splits fall out of `telemetry.sectors` and the existing `crossings` for free. |
| **IDEA-108** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **Records belong to the exact chain, not the endpoint pair.** `A→C` and `A→B→C` are different races over different ground; a record that conflated them would be false. The route id must derive from the **whole ordered sequence** — today `routeId` is `root_<start>__to__<finish>`, which cannot distinguish them. SP's `deriveRootId(startNodeId, finishNodeId, nodeIds)` already took `nodeIds` for exactly this reason. **Load-bearing:** `routeRecordSec` / `previousBestSec` are supplied to B-089 race tags as *facts*, so a wrong key silently corrupts personal bests and route records. Reverse routes: geometry derives by reversing `nodes`, but **identity does not** — `The Cut` and `Reverse Cut` are already two separate named roots, which is the precedent. Authored **gates never auto-reverse** (a gate is a directed line and `gates.crossed` tests an approach-side sign flip). | agent audit, 2026-09-22 | shaped | with IDEA-107 | Validation for a chain: every id exists (report *which index*, as SP did); ≥2 after collapsing consecutive duplicates; ≥2 distinct; no non-consecutive repeat unless first == last (a deliberate loop); **max chain length ~8**; a minimum leg length so splits cannot be farmed; total distance ceiling; **node ids only, never coordinates**. `North Tunnel → Gold Niwaki Plaza → Trench` passes all of these. |
| **IDEA-109** | **SYS** | 2026-09-22 | **Concurrent races, disjoint by START region.** Owner: *"multiple races can and should be able to run concurrently, not just 1 every 20 mins. prioritize avoiding overlap of regions at the same time."* **The infrastructure already exists** — `calendar.lua` already loops every series through `Cal.materialize`, so concurrency needs more series or one series emitting several events per slot, not a new scheduler. Two real gaps: **event ids collide** (`seriesId@startsAt` is unique only at one event per slot — the route must fold into the id), and **nothing coordinates regions across series** (disjointness is global; `materialize` is pure and per-series). | owner, 2026-09-22 | shaped | after IDEA-104 | **Measured against the ten routes: requiring no shared region at all yields only ~2 concurrent events** — Heywood appears in 6 of 10 and starves everything. Requiring only **disjoint start regions yields 5** (starts are Westbrook ×2, Heywood ×4, City Center, Watson ×2, Pacifica). Start-disjoint is also the truer rule: **players converge at the venue**, so the start is where overlap actually costs; two races crossing the same district mid-route is atmosphere, not a collision. Recommend start-region-disjoint with finish-region disjointness as a soft tiebreak. |
| **IDEA-110** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **Promotion: an ephemeral chain becomes an official route with no change of abstraction.** Same ordered node list, same resolve path; promotion is a provenance and curation change — give it a `displayName` and description, optionally author gates per leg, clear the ephemeral marker, persist it. **This is the strongest argument for chain-first:** the thing a player builds ad hoc and the thing NCM ships as official are the same object at different life stages. | agent audit, 2026-09-22 | shaped | after IDEA-106 | Keeps the owner's constraint intact — ephemeral routes never touch the calendar or `custom_route` until someone deliberately promotes them. |


## Championships as the organizing principle (2026-09-22, owner)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-111** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **PARENT — the CHAMPIONSHIP is the organizing principle, not route cadence.** `NODE(S) → ROUTE → CHAMPIONSHIP → SCHEDULED EVENT → EVENT INSTANCE`. Provisional catalogue: NCM City Cup (20 min), MaiMai Cup (30 min), Night City GT (hourly), Badlands Rallycross (90 min), Hypercar (3 h), NCX World Championship (explicit calendar). Independent cadences overlap on purpose; the Race Guide becomes **a motorsport programming guide rather than a countdown dispenser**. Audit: [07_NODE_NETWORK.md](07_NODE_NETWORK.md) Addendum C. **Finding: the championship primitive already exists and is called `series`** — it already owns cadence, route pool, discipline, format, class, ruleset, capacity and entry window, and no route owns any sporting rule. `Cal.materialize` is per-series, `Cal.merge` merges many, and the server already loops `seriesList`. **Adding five championships is pack data, not code.** | owner, 2026-09-22 | **BUILT 2026-09-22 (5 of 6), offline green, undeployed** | deploy 11; QA §O | `eventId = seriesId@startsAt` **already survives concurrency** — two championships at 10:00 give two distinct ids. Collisions only arise if one series emits several events per slot, which is why championship-based concurrency is the right mechanism. **Addendum B's region-disjoint scheduler is withdrawn as the primary mechanism**; owner: *"if we have too much heywood its fine… should not be rigid."* Region spacing becomes a tiebreak inside a championship's route rotation. |
| **IDEA-112** | **SYS** | 2026-09-22 | **THE ONE HARD CONCURRENCY BLOCKER: `GlobalState.ncmSession` is a single slot.** `publish()` does `GlobalState.ncmSession = v` for **every** session, so two live races make every driver's HUD flicker between them, last writer wins. **A precedent fix already exists in the same file:** practice publishes to `GlobalState.ncmPractice` as a **map per event**, and the client's `pickView()` already scans it for the view the local driver is an entrant of. Convert races to `ncmSessions[sessionId]` and extend `pickView`. | agent audit, 2026-09-22 | **BUILT 2026-09-22, offline green, undeployed** (L69: not done until a live PASS) | deploy 11; QA §L | Everything else audited clean: `sessions`/`plans`/`byEvent`/`pickups` are all keyed maps, `tick()` loops every session, settlement and progression are per-rec, **nothing locks a route** so the same route can host two championships. Remaining smaller items: venue dressing is a singleton (`dressUp(reason)` takes no venue — affects sanctioned events only), `publishCount = 3` caps the guide, and `pickView`'s fallback needs a spectator policy. Participant isolation is **already correct** (`pickView` requires entrant status; KB-37 stops one driver joining two). |
| **IDEA-113** | **SYS** | 2026-09-22 | **RESOLVED BY L83: bring-your-own, refusal not substitution.** **Vehicle eligibility: declared, expressive, never once called.** `competition.class.eligible(classId, record)` exists with per-class record lists and has **zero call sites**; `enterPlayer` never inspects what you are driving. Arbitrary machinery is prevented only incidentally, because `NcmGarage.issue` hands you a car. **`data.vehicle_pool` is already the data-driven catalogue the brief asks for** — `Muscle/Sport/Tech/Nomad/Bike/Formula` × `prestige 1..5` × free-form `tags` — and expresses all six championships without new fields (MaiMai is one record; City Cup is prestige ≤2; GT is prestige ≥4 + `sport`/`race`; Hypercar is `halo`; Rallycross is `Nomad`). **One gap:** the brief names manufacturer families (Mizutani, Quadra, Herrera, Porsche). Maker is embedded in record ids but **a parsed id is not a catalogue** — add an explicit `make` field rather than string-matching ids. | agent audit, 2026-09-22 | **BUILT 2026-09-22, offline green, undeployed** | deploy 11; QA §N | **This is the second rule in this module family that nothing invokes** — the first was the performance ceiling (KB-30, `performanceFor` had no caller either). Pattern worth naming: *core declares the rule, the host forgets to ask.* Not expressible as a core test; has to be a review habit. Also: `classId` today conflates **BoP ceiling** and **eligible records**; a championship needs eligibility, and a ceiling is a separate concern several championships may share. Split them. |
| **IDEA-114** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **Explicit calendar scheduling — the only genuinely new primitive in the brief.** `validateSeries` hard-requires `cadence.everyMinutes >= 1`, so a championship with real dates (NCX World Championship) **cannot be expressed today**. Model `schedule` as a tagged union: `{ kind = "interval", everyMinutes, offsetMinutes }` or `{ kind = "calendar", starts = { … } }`, and keep `materialize` pure and deterministic for both so re-materialising never duplicates. | agent audit, 2026-09-22 | shaped | after the five interval championships ship | Everything else the brief asks for is data; this is the one place the calendar core must change. |


## Ownership as the prerequisite for eligibility (2026-09-22)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-115** | **F0** | 2026-09-22 | **The ownership ledger does not exist, and it is the real prerequisite for L83.** L55 describes `owned_vehicle` rows per driver; **no such table exists in any migration.** The only garage table is `issued_vehicle`, whose `model` column is already `ENUM('issued','loaner','owned','byo')` — **the vocabulary for the end state was reserved and the ledger behind it was never built**, and only `'issued'` is ever written. Eligibility (`competition.class.eligible`) is ready and waiting; ownership is the missing half. Needed before "bring your own car" can be enforced at all. | agent audit, 2026-09-22, following L83 | **BUILT 2026-09-22, offline green, undeployed** | deploy 11; QA §M | **Two checks, two refusals:** *eligible* ("MaiMai Cup is one-make — you need a Makigai MaiMai") and *owned* ("You don't own one yet"). Collapsing them into "you can't enter" throws away the only useful part — whether the fix is *switch cars* or *go and earn one*. |
| **IDEA-116** | **SYS** | 2026-09-22 | **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-22 by the owner: seed a real garage instead of building a bypass.** *"any reason i cant have all cars needed already owned?"* — none, and it is strictly better: seeding real `owned_vehicle` rows exercises the real check, where a loaner flag would only ever exercise the flag, and there is no bypass left that could ship by accident. `ncm.grant <pid> all` is the seeding path and records `acquired_via = 'seed'`, so a seeded car stays distinguishable from an earned one forever. **Original proposal, kept for the reasoning:** **The QA spawn path must not satisfy the ownership check by accident.** L83 permits NCM-spawned cars while testing. That is exactly the shape of bypass that lets a rule rot unobserved — which already happened twice in this module family (KB-30's ceiling and `eligible` itself: declared, correct, never asked). Proposal: the QA spawn writes `model = 'loaner'`; the ownership check accepts loaners **only** while a host-kit flag is on; and it **logs every time it does**. A silent bypass is indistinguishable from no rule. | agent audit, 2026-09-22 | shaped | with IDEA-115 | Also names where the refusal belongs: **the venue option list (IDEA-095) already has the right shape** — `competition.venue_options` declines options and records `reasons`, and the rule is already *absent, not greyed*. `ENTER RACE` should simply not appear for a driver who cannot comply, with the reason in the host log and the *fix* stated where the player reads it. No new surface needed. |


## Economy: money, purses, entry fees and recovery (2026-09-22, owner)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-117** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **PARENT — the economy primitive.** *"Finishing races keeps you alive. Performing well moves you forward. Expensive ambitions pull money back out. Mistakes create risk. Going broke gives you a road back, not free money."* Loop: race → earn → spend on automotive life → race. Money exists to create **opportunity cost**, not to be an RPG economy. Full audit: [08_ECONOMY.md](08_ECONOMY.md). **Finding: the ledger already exists and settlement already writes to it.** L21 said "start money-free; build the ledger now" and that was actually done — migration 004 has a `ledger` table (driver/server/kind/amount/currency `eddies`/ref/note) and `race.lua` writes a `race_result` line **per driver per race with amount 0** today. `entry.fee`, `issued_vehicle.cost` and `provisioning.cost` all exist and are all hardcoded 0 with "L21: money-free first world". **The seams were left open on purpose; the work is switching them on.** | owner brief + agent audit, 2026-09-22 | **audited; decisions taken (L84), implementation HELD (L85)** | **blocked on Deploy 11 validation** | Entry fees are **sinks**, purses are **faucets**, and they are tuned independently — the purse is never a redistribution of collected fees, or low-population races become worthless and big fields inflate. Practice already writes no ledger line at all (the write is guarded to `kind == "race"`), so "practice is not a faucet" is already structurally true. |
| **IDEA-118** | **F0** | 2026-09-22 | **THE BLOCKER: the ledger has no idempotency key, and nothing may write a non-zero amount until it does.** `PRIMARY KEY (ledger_id)` is auto-increment; the only other index is `(ncm_driver_id, created_at)`. Nothing prevents the same row twice. Harmless while every amount is 0; **the moment a purse is non-zero, a settlement retry pays it twice.** This is precisely KB-38's shape — where `INSERT IGNORE` failed to dedupe because the unique key included the session id — except that bug cost a wrong badge and this one costs money and is invisible until someone audits balances. Fix: `idem_key VARCHAR(190) UNIQUE`, plus typed `event_id` / `session_id` / `series_id` columns so telemetry groups without parsing ids (the IDEA-113 "a parsed id is not a catalogue" lesson). Callers build keys deterministically: `entry:<eventId>:<driverId>`, `payout:<sessionId>:<driverId>`, `starter:<driverId>:<serverId>`. | agent audit, 2026-09-22 | **specified; hard prerequisite under L85** | **first, before any amount is non-zero** | Balance is `SUM(amount)`, cached per connected driver like `NcmOwnership` does, **never a `balance` column** — a derived balance cannot drift from its own history, and "why does this player have this balance" is answerable by definition. |
| **IDEA-119** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **Championship economics as a payout TABLE, not a universal formula.** An `economy` block beside `eligibility` on each series: `entryFee`, `purse = { P1, P2, P3 }`, `completion` (every classified finisher), `dnf`, `fieldBonus = { perEntrant, max }`, `timeTrial = { completion, personalBest, dailyCompletions }`. A table is readable, showable to the player verbatim, and tunable per championship without re-deriving algebra; a formula only earns its place where a table cannot express the shape, which here is the capped field bonus — two numbers. **Charge at check-in, not at interest** (interest is a soft list with no reservation, L48/P52 — browsing must not cost money), and check-in already has the KB-37 busy guard and the L83 eligibility guard in front of it, plus `ux_entry_event_driver` as a natural idempotency anchor. **Pay at settlement**, replacing the existing zero-amount line rather than adding a second. | owner brief + audit, 2026-09-22 | **specified (L84 c/d/e/f/g)** | with IDEA-117, after the hold lifts | Refund policy in the audit §3 as a table. The only case needing new machinery is **"resource restart with the event unresolved" → refund by boot sweep** — the one the owner named: we must not eat a fee because the server failed. Idempotent by construction, so running it twice is free. |
| **IDEA-120** | **SYS** | 2026-09-22 | **THE MAIMINES — the recovery seam already exists, and it is one word.** *"Hey choom. Heard you're down on your luck. I'll front you a MaiMai. Don't go wrecking it."* A player may reach €$0; their ability to participate must never permanently reach zero, and the answer is **a way to earn again, not free cash**. `issued_vehicle.model` is already `ENUM('issued','loaner','owned','byo')` — a loaner is an `issued_vehicle` row with `model = 'loaner'` and **no** `owned_vehicle` row. Nothing needs designing now beyond not conflating the two tables: `NcmOwnership.owns` must keep meaning *owns*, and eligibility must eventually ask "owns **or** has on loan". That is a one-line change later precisely because the tables are separate. Recovery trigger — balance at zero **and** no eligible car — is answerable from the ledger plus the garage once the first slice exists. | owner, 2026-09-22 | **seam preserved by L84(j)**; no implementation in the first slice | after the first economy slice | Loaner must not be sellable, transferable or convertible to capital, and wrecking it must not softlock. All of that is expressible because a loaner is not owned. |
| **IDEA-121** | **F0** | 2026-09-22 | **A competing wallet already exists on the same server.** `open77_freeroam/server/persistence.lua` keeps `players.money BIGINT`, keyed by **platform identifier**, and logs it on every join (`A joined (money=0)`). It is a bare balance column with no ledger. NCM's economy is keyed by `ncm_driver_id` and is per-server career state (L54/L55). **Two money systems, two identity models.** Recommend they never meet, stated explicitly — otherwise someone eventually "fixes" the fact that a player has two balances. | agent audit, 2026-09-22 | **DECIDED — L84(b): they never meet; L84(c) resolves L55** | closed | Also flagged: **L55 says "prize/fee schedules live on rulesets"** while the owner now says championship/event. Rulesets carry sporting rules and nothing economic today, so this is a live contradiction needing one sentence in the decision record. Recommend the championship, matching where `eligibility` went. |


## Platform stress test and grid density (2026-09-22)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-122** | **OPS** | 2026-09-22 | **Measure NCM's own grid density before TW2 sizing, and treat field size as a tunable rather than an ambition.** KB-50: the platform's stress test found vehicle sync degrades with many vehicles concentrated in a small area, and a race grid is the densest arrangement on the platform. **The honest position is that we do not know our own numbers** — NCM has never fielded more than two cars, so every density claim about TW2 is untested. Proposal, in order: (1) use the host kit to spawn a 20-car grid with no drivers and simply *look* at it — `ncm.scenario` and the grid-car path already exist and this costs one session; (2) record client FPS at the grid, at lights-out, and through turn one; (3) repeat at 12 and at 8 to find where it stops being comfortable; (4) set each championship's `capacity` from what we measured, not from L60's ambition. | agent, 2026-09-22, from the OPEN//77 stress-test report | shaped | **before TW2 sizing; after Deploy 11 QA** | **L60 says TW2 aims at 20 cars and "must degrade gracefully below 20" — that instinct now has a platform reason behind it, not just a turnout worry.** Cheapest mitigations are all existing knobs (grid spacing, capacity, the 150 ms spawn stagger, venue dressing volume). **Rolling starts are structurally less dense than standing grids** — ~300 m of formation instead of a 70 m box — which makes KB-46's missing ring route more valuable than it looked. |
| **IDEA-123** | **UP** | 2026-09-22 | **Raise NCM's density case with the OPEN//77 devs while they are actively working on vehicle sync.** They named vehicle concentration as the one weak spot and said they are improving the sync protocol and reducing client-side sync workload. NCM is plausibly their most demanding vehicle-density consumer with a **concrete, describable use case**: N stationary networked vehicles in a ~70 m × 5 m box, held through a countdown, then accelerating together along the same line. That is a better bug report than "racing feels laggy", and it arrives while the work is open rather than after it ships. Pairs with the two asks already outstanding (AI speed cap 55 m/s, identity portability). | agent, 2026-09-22 | raw | owner to raise, alongside the existing dev asks | They will publish server-side stress reports and performance data — read those first so the ask is specific. |

Not known to be causing the mod verdicts — the refusal is master-side and predates the gap — but reasoning about platform behaviour across a version mismatch is how false findings get written down. | agent, 2026-09-22 | raw | before the next platform investigation | Upgrade path is proven now: `tools/staging/upgrade-open77.sh` + **`tools/staging/check-load-list.py` before restarting** (KB-51). |


## Restart discipline (2026-09-22, owner)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-130** | **OPS** | 2026-09-22 | **Close the restart-countdown seam so the announce and the restart are one action.** L90 is implemented in two halves today: `ncm.restart [seconds]` (NCM, run from Warden's Live Console — broadcasts at 60/30/10/5 with `ncm.restart.cancel` to call it off) and `tools/staging/restart-with-warning.sh` (the box — counts down, restarts, waits for the service, checks the boot line). **NCM cannot restart its own host**, so the two must currently be started together by hand, which is exactly the kind of two-step a tired operator skips at 2am. **Full automation is possible and I deliberately did not guess at it:** the platform declares `filesystem.read` / `filesystem.write` permissions, so a script could drop a marker file for NCM to poll — but **nothing in the shipped resources uses either permission and the API shape is undocumented**. Guessing `Open77.fs.read` vs `Open77.filesystem.readFile` and wrapping it in `pcall` would fail silently, which is the exact failure mode that has bitten this codebase repeatedly (KB-30's uncalled ceiling, KB-8's non-matching regex, the swallowed guide publish). Either confirm the API against build-pinned cards, or drive the announce through Warden's API with a token. | owner L90 + agent, 2026-09-22 | **half built**; automation shaped | with the next ops pass | Also fold a **whitelist check** into the post-upgrade steps (KB-52) — that is now two things one upgrade silently broke, after KB-51. |


## First-run experience for walk-in visitors (2026-09-22, owner)

| ID | Class | Added | Idea | Source | State | Lands in | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **IDEA-131** | **UI** | 2026-09-22 | **Be friendly to strangers: one small welcome, honest about alpha.** Owner, after a third walk-in: *"embrace friendliness and have a small toast like Need a vehicle? type xxxx into chat so if they do spawn in they actually know how to drive around. obviously we are alpha status ourselves so be clear this is a wip."* **Built 2026-09-22:** fires **once**, for a newly minted driver only, 6 s after arrival so the identity toast lands first — a toast (`NCM · ALPHA` / *"Racing server, half-built and being worked on live. Need a car? Type /ncm car Sport"*) plus two chat lines naming the alpha state, the car command with a concrete class, `/ncm return`, and F6 for races. | owner, 2026-09-22 | **built, offline green, undeployed** | next deploy | Deliberately once-only and minted-only: a returning visitor already knows, and an unsolicited card on every join is exactly what IDEA-127 removed — the same mistake in friendlier words. The alpha line also pairs with **L90**: if a visitor meets a restart countdown, it will not come out of nowhere. |

| **IDEA-139** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **One generalized vehicle transaction: CAR ↔ CAR, CAR ↔ EDDIES, CAR + EDDIES ↔ CAR + EDDIES.** Not two systems. Never money without the car; never one leg without the other. | owner spec 1/3, 2026-09-22 | blocked (persistent assetId) | after asset identity | **Hard-blocked, and the spec says do not fake it (its point 7).** `owned_vehicle` is keyed `(driver, server, record)` — one row per record — which literally cannot express "I own two Caliburns", and §G requires exactly that. | **IDEA-140** | **INV** | 2026-09-22 | **Ownership changes only through explicit authoritative NCM transactions. | **IDEA-141** | **F0** | 2026-09-22 | **Persistent `assetId`: which actual example of a machine this is.** `vehicleKey` answers *what kind of machine*; `assetId` answers *which one*. | **IDEA-144** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **Authored supply policy per vehicleKey.** Acquisition channel, starting inventory, batch size, cadence, inventory cap, optional lifetime cap, eligibility, factory-configuration distribution, production state (continuous / periodic / seasonal / authored / discontinued). **Explicitly not rarity labels** — no COMMON/RARE/EPIC/LEGENDARY. Scarcity emerges from factual supply. | owner spec 2/3, 2026-09-22 | shaped | supply spine | Numbers stay authored and tunable; the architecture is the deliverable, not the balance. | **IDEA-145** | **INV** | 2026-09-22 | **Discrete allocation ticks, never a regenerating decimal.** `R34: batchSize 4, cadence weekly, inventoryCap 16` — at the tick, up to four NEW persistent assets may enter primary inventory subject to caps. | owner spec 2/3, 2026-09-22 | shaped | supply spine | **The highest-risk piece in spec 2.** A tick that runs twice — restart, double-schedule, clock skew — mints duplicate cars and permanently violates the conservation rule (IDEA-149); you cannot un-mint a car someone already bought. | **IDEA-146** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **Inventory cap and lifetime cap are different, and the throttle happens before minting.** Inventory cap = unsold examples allowed to accumulate (MaiMai 500: if 500 unsold, the next allocation creates **zero**). | **IDEA-147** | **INV** | 2026-09-22 | **Production is authored; demand is emergent.** NCM may observe inventory, sales rate, sellout frequency, unfulfilled interest, market activity and prices — but strictly as operator signals. **No automatic "high demand → produce more".** Scarcity may be intentional gameplay. | owner spec 2/3, 2026-09-22 | shaped | architectural invariant | The one automatic behaviour that must never be built. | **IDEA-148** | **F1** | 2026-09-22 | **Factory configuration is part of the allocation, not a purchase dropdown.** An allocation of 12 is *6 red, 3 yellow, 2 baby blue, 1 purple* — those are the actual twelve cars. | **IDEA-164** | **INV** | 2026-09-22 | **Settlement must be idempotent and auditable.** Money must not duplicate or disappear across retries and restarts; every credit and debit traces to an authoritative sporting input. | owner spec 3/3, 2026-09-22 | shaped | with the economy slice | **Already the project's own position** — L85 hold #1 makes duplicate charge, duplicate settlement and restart/retry *gate tests*. ## How to use this file

- **Adding:** append a row with the next free ID, bump the "next free ID" line at the top, state `raw`. Do not
  stop to design it. One sentence is enough.
- **Promoting:** when an idea gets a design or an owner answer it becomes `shaped`; when it enters a batch or
  milestone it becomes `scheduled` and the "Lands in" column is filled.
- **Finishing:** `built` when code and offline proof exist; `validated` only when a live PASS is logged in
  `wp/MASTER_TEST.md`. `built` is not done (L69).
- **Dropping:** never delete. Set `dropped (reason)` so the next session does not rediscover and re-argue it.

## Completed — moved here 2026-09-24, nothing purged

Rows land here when they are **done**: built and live-verified, answered, accepted into a decision, or struck.
They keep their ID, their text and their original section heading, because why a thing was asked for is part of
the record. Nothing is ever deleted (L68). A row that is *built but not yet live-verified* stays above — it is
not done until a live PASS sits beside it (L69).

They were right on both counts. **Verified:** `RegisterCommand("ncm.car")` refuses unless `source == 0` (server console); the single choke point `NcmGarage.command(playerId, args, authorized)` refuses `sub == "car"` whenever `authorized` is falsy **and logs it as an L102 refusal**; and both player-reachable routes -- the `/ncm` chat dispatcher (`main.lua:626`) and the `ncm:cmd` net event -- call it with **no** `authorized` argument. Only two callers pass `true`: the operator console and `reissueLast`, the server-side reconnect restoration no client can reach. Production confirms it: **no loaner issued since 2026-09-23, none today.** **What remains true, and is all that `IDEA-307` should ever have said:** an operator-issued car and an NCX team car are still non-assets, so `attribution_basis = 'unknown'` stays reachable **by operator action, never by player action**. That is `IDEA-238`'s territory, not an economy hole. **Why this happened:** I found `issued_vehicle` rows and the `/ncm car` code path and concluded loaners were open, without checking the authorization argument or searching the decision record for a governing L-line. L102 is explicit, was recorded the day before, and says exactly this. A grep for the mechanism is not a check of the rule. |
| IDEA-308 | **UI** | 2026-09-24 | **Anything worth a toast must also reach the open panel.** The owner missed a discovery firing because the toast drew behind F6, and names event interest and future toasts as the same case. | owner, 2026-09-24, after the first live discovery PASS | **shaped -- the NCM-half is cheap and already proven** | `ncm:*` -> `panel:*` pattern; extends the KB-73 fix | **The goal is right; the stated mechanism is not ours.** `Open77.notifications` is **0 client / 5 server** -- NCM sends a notification and the platform draws it, with no client-side handle to reposition or re-layer it (compare `Open77.blips`, 23 client). So raising z-order over an NCM WebUI page is a platform ask (`IDEA-309`), not a local fix. **KB-73 already solved this exact shape the other way**: CALL VEHICLE outcomes now reach the PAGE as well as the toast, and that shipped. Generalise it -- one small server-side helper that, when a player has a panel open, mirrors the notification into the page. **Discovery is the best possible first case and is arguably better in-panel than as an overlay**: the Machine Index row or Market card can light up in place, which a toast floating over the panel could never do. |
| IDEA-309 | **UP** | 2026-09-24 | **Platform ask: notification layering/priority above a focused WebUI page** -- or any client-side handle on a notification at all. | arising from `IDEA-308`, 2026-09-24 | **raw -- dev ask, not blocking** | OPEN//77 | Measured against the captured namespace inventory: `Open77.notifications: 0 client, 5 server`. There is no client surface, so a resource cannot raise, reposition or re-render its own toast relative to its own page. This is the root cause behind KB-73 (a toast behind the panel made a working button look dead), KB-91 (NCM's race graph and NCM's own toasts competing for the same edge) and now the missed discovery. **NCM has a workable answer without it** (`IDEA-308`), so this is a quality ask rather than a blocker -- but it affects every toast NCM will ever send. |
| IDEA-310 | **UI** | 2026-09-24 | **The instrument cluster should be an authored period/class piece, not one generic readout.** A persistent panel that is on screen whenever you are in the machine deserves to look like that machine's era and purpose. Owner's palette: **mechanical rolling drums** (with a mechanical trip meter), **early electronic LCD**, **modern digital cluster**, **Cyberpunk OEM digital**, **motorsport display**. | owner, 2026-09-24, while driving a purchased car | **raw -- EXPLICITLY DEFERRED by the owner ("for later though")** | extends `IDEA-230`; §AG7 | Captured when spoken (L68), **not scheduled**. This is UI and waits behind the foundational work by the owner's own direction. One note worth keeping for whoever builds it: the cluster is already **bound to the assetId**, not to the driver or the session, so a per-class or per-era treatment is a presentation choice over data that already identifies the exact machine -- it needs no new substrate. The style would presumably key off the catalogue's class/tier, or eventually the machine's own authored character. Rolling-drum digits are the only one implying real animation work. |
| IDEA-311 | **F0** | 2026-09-24 | **`world_id` does not need a 28-table migration: `server_id` already IS the world id.** `24_FOUNDATION_RECONCILIATION.md` section 9 listed M5 as adding `world_id` across 28 tables. That was over-scoped. | found while starting the schema batch, 2026-09-24 | **shaped -- supersedes M5 in `24` section 9** | 16_ENVIRONMENTS.md; L112 | L112 already defines `serverId` as *"an opaque identity, not a statement about environment"* that *"must never change on the live world"* -- permanent, opaque, non-semantic. **That is a world identity in everything but name.** What does not exist is a RUNTIME/instance id, and nothing needs one until multiple processes serve one world. Q35 asked for the distinction *"where reasonably inexpensive"*; the inexpensive version is to state that `server_id` means WORLD, and to add `runtime_id` later on the few operational rows that would care (sessions, events), not on 28 canonical tables. **Saves the largest and riskiest migration in the Stage 1 plan for no loss of the preparedness the owner asked for.** |
| IDEA-312 | **F0** | 2026-09-24 | **Entry checks that you OWN an eligible model; it never checks which machine you are actually in.** So a driver can enter on the strength of owning one car and go racing in another, and nothing anywhere notices. | owner question, 2026-09-24: *"do we actually check what car you have and are sat in at the enter gate of a race, or do we just check that you generally own one?"* | **BUILT 2026-09-24 (migrations 026 + rules), offline green; live validation owed** | competition.lua:124; calendar.lua:426; guide.lua:347 | **Traced, not recalled.** `NcmCompetition.carRefusal(e, driverId)` calls `NcmOwnership.records(driverId)` -- **record ids, deduplicated, so owning two MaiMais is one option not two** -- and asks `Eligibility.check` whether the spec admits any. The record that made you eligible is **computed and thrown away**: `decision.record` is never stored, and `entry` has no vehicle column. `carRefusal` has exactly two callers, ENTER and the Guide display; **nothing runs at grid, at green, or during the race.** `gridCars.record` comes from `Teams.carForSeat`, which is team equipment for organized events, not the driver's own machine. **This is an eligibility gap, not an attribution gap** -- Stage 0's `asset_id` is truthful about what was driven either way. **Recommended order:** now that green captures the fielded machine, *log* the disagreement between eligible-by-owning-X and raced-in-Y before writing any rule, because nobody knows yet whether it happens. Then (b) validate at green that the fielded record is admitted, and only later (a) bind entry to a specific asset the way real motorsport does -- you enter a car, with a number. **OWNER OVERRULED THE MEASURE-FIRST RECOMMENDATION, 2026-09-24, and was right:** *"who is racing that we are risking kicking off? just me, the person developing this."* Caution about kicking players off a grid is empty when the only racer is the developer. **Built both (b) and (a):** an entry now names a physical machine (`entry.asset_id`) and carries a car number, preferring the machine the driver drove up in and the number they last raced under; and just after green, a driver demonstrably sitting in a machine other than the one they entered is refused the start. Refuses only on an **explicit contradiction** -- an unknown seat is not a violation (KB-72). |
| IDEA-313 | **OPS** | 2026-09-24 | **A static gate for Lua local-ordering**: a function that references a `local` defined LATER in the same file silently captures a nil global instead. | fifth instance, caught during `IDEA-312`, 2026-09-24 | **raw -- recommended, not built** | tools/ncm-test.ps1 | Instances so far: KB-81(a) `NcmClientRaceTarget` above `local gpsTarget`; three more the same day recorded in L113's cross-scope work; and `NcmRace.enforceEnteredMachine` defined 160 lines above `local function handle`, which would have failed at runtime and been **swallowed by the pcall that calls it**. **`luac -p` cannot see this** -- the file is syntactically perfect. `tools/test-cross-scope.lua` catches the sibling defect (globals across VMs) and proves the shape of the fix. A heuristic version is cheap: collect `local function NAME` line numbers per file, then flag a reference to NAME from a function body that begins earlier. Scoping makes a perfect version hard; the common case is the one that keeps biting. |

So the entry resolver needs a *fieldable set* -- machines owned by the driver **plus** machines their team seat assigns them -- not an ownership list. Cheap to fix now while team cars have no assets and nothing can break; a live defect on the day M6 runs, which is the worst possible time to find it. |

Prose did not stop that. The mechanically testable form: **no enum in `ncm/core/vocabulary/` may contain a value naming a USE rather than a fact** -- `team_equipment`, `loaner`, `grid_car`, `league_car`. Candidate for the invariant register (`IDEA-068`-style) and a grep-level gate assertion. |

My suggestion, not a ruling: the driver's odometer shows the **model** ordinal (`HELLA #0004`) because that is the number an owner feels -- the fourth Hella ever made -- while the global NCM serial belongs on the Machine Profile beside provenance. Worth settling once, because every future surface that shows a machine inherits it. |
